Discuss Compliance Automation Scaling.

Based on the Phoenix Rising archives, Compliance Automation Scaling is not treated as a bureaucratic layer but as a hard-coded architectural "Iron Spine" designed to support the 1 Billion VND valuation target. The system transitions from "Governance Theater" (documents) to "Operational Reality" (code) by embedding compliance directly into the execution pipelines via "Drones," "Durable Objects," and "Fail-Closed" logic.
1. The Core Philosophy: Governance as Code
The foundation of scaling compliance is the rejection of human trust in favor of cryptographic certainty. The system operates under the "Ledger is Law" axiom, meaning if an event is not appended to the immutable D1 SQLite ledger, it did not happen.
• Fail-Closed Architecture: To scale safely, the system uses a "Fail-Closed" protocol. If any input is ambiguous, unverified, or detects a drift greater than 5%, the system halts execution immediately rather than guessing. This prevents compliance risks from cascading during high-velocity operations.
• The Governance Latch: Compliance is enforced by "Agent-97 (OB1)," the Auditor, who possesses the power to trigger a "System Halt" if drift is detected, ensuring that scaling revenue does not outpace security.
2. The Two-Ledger Topology for Privacy Scaling
To manage the tension between high-volume data ingestion (audio/transcripts) and strict governance, the system utilizes a split-ledger architecture:
• Evidence Ledger: Handles high-volume, "messy" inputs like raw classroom audio and transcripts. This stream is subject to heavy redaction and hashing.
• Operational Ledger: A low-volume, high-integrity ledger for "Sacred State" mutations like payments, credentials, and authorization decisions.
• The Scaling Rule: Operational events reference Evidence events by hash but never mix the streams. This prevents "evidence spam" from clogging the governance spine and ensures that sensitive data in the Evidence Ledger can be managed or purged without breaking the financial history in the Operational Ledger.
3. Automated Redaction and K12 Compliance
As the system scales into the educational sector (NET/K12), compliance with data privacy (FERPA/Vietnam "Face-Safe" constraints) is automated via the "Redaction Pipeline".
• Streaming Transcription: To comply with K12 regulations, the system refactors audio processing from "Load-then-Process" to "Streaming Transcription" using 5-second chunks. This ensures full audio files are never held in memory, minimizing PII retention risks.
• Pre-Write Scrubbing: A mandatory privacy filter runs before any ledger entry to scrub student PII (names, faces) from capture streams.
• The "Face-Safe" Constraint: In the Vietnamese context, protecting student identity is a cultural and legal constraint. The automation explicitly tags and redacts this data to ensure the "Empire" can scale without social liability.
4. Drone Swarms and Autonomous Monitoring
Scaling compliance is achieved by deploying "Drones"—stateless execution units—that continuously monitor the system's health and integrity.
• Specific Compliance Drones:
    ◦ Compliance Collector: A drone dedicated to gathering audit evidence.
    ◦ Schema Watcher: Detects if data structures drift from the "Canon".
    ◦ Ingest Validator: Validates payloads against strict JSON schemas before they touch the ledger.
• The Insight Drone: Moves from scripted ingestion to self-directed mining with guardrails, ensuring that revenue signals are extracted legally and accurately.
• Automated Invoicing: Monthly triggers scan student progress and automatically generate/email PDF invoices, removing human error from financial compliance.
5. Verification and The Omega Seal
To prove compliance at scale, the system uses cryptographic verification rather than manual checks.
• The Omega Seal: A cryptographic hash (SHA-256) that locks the system state (e.g., Ledger Height 62) and validates that the code, ledger, and assets match the authorized "Canon".
• Verifychain: A gate that runs post-deployment to ensure the ledger height is monotonic (always increasing) and the chain is unbroken. If verification fails, the pipeline halts.
• Audit Bundles: The system can generate a PHOENIX_AUDIT_BUNDLE_.zip containing hashed ledger snapshots and evidence manifests, allowing external auditors to verify the system without needing "trust".
6. The Incident Ladder
To handle failures without total collapse, the system scales its response via the "Incident Ladder":
• Level 1 (Caution): Blocks external calls but allows "Shadow" (simulation) runs.
• Level 2 (Freeze): Read-only mode; all writes are rejected.
• Level 3 (Kill): Zero-trust shutdown requiring physical key rotation.
This graduated response ensures that compliance breaches are contained instantly while allowing non-critical read operations to continue.
Discuss SHA-256 hash algorithms.

Within the Phoenix architecture, SHA-256 is not merely a hashing algorithm; it is the "Omega Seal," the mathematical enforcement of the "Ledger is Law" axiom. It serves as the primary mechanism for establishing immutable history, verifying evidence, and preventing "Drift" (unauthorized changes) across the 1 Billion VND revenue engine.
1. The Physics of the "Immortal Stitch" (Hash Chaining)
The core ledger utilizes a recursive SHA-256 hash chain to ensure linearity. Every block’s existence is mathematically dependent on the block preceding it. The formula mandated by the architecture is:
B 
n
​
 =SHA-256(Canonical(Payload 
n
​
 +Hash(B 
n−1
​
 )))
• Mechanism: The system snapshots the current "Head Hash" (Time Lock) and combines it with the "Payload Hash" (Content Lock).
• Linearity: This creates an unbroken chain from the "Genesis Block" (Block 0) to the present. If a single byte of history is altered, the hashes mismatch, breaking the chain and triggering a Fail-Closed event (System Halt).
• The Anakin Reflex: In high-velocity scenarios (e.g., multiple "Tiger Tier" payments), if the Head Hash moves during processing (Head Drift), the CAS-Retry engine immediately recalculates the SHA-256 hash with the new parent to "stitch" the transaction into the timeline without data loss.
2. Canonicalization (RFC 8785)
To ensure the SHA-256 hash is deterministic across different environments (e.g., local WSL vs. Cloudflare Workers), the system enforces Canonical JSON (RFC 8785) before hashing.
• The Rule: A standard JSON.stringify is insufficient because key order is not guaranteed.
• The Fix: The system lexicographically sorts object keys and normalizes number formatting (IEEE 754) before passing the string to the SHA-256 function.
• Result: This guarantees that { "a": 1, "b": 2 } and { "b": 2, "a": 1 } produce the exact same fingerprint, eliminating "format drift" that could invalidate the chain.
3. CT-Style Merkle Trees (Audit Integrity)
For the 500-step execution map and audit checklists, the system utilizes a Certificate Transparency (CT) style Merkle Tree (RFC 6962/9162) rather than a simple hash list.
• Domain Separation: To prevent second-preimage attacks, the hashing logic applies specific prefixes:
    ◦ Leaves: SHA-256(0x00 || payload).
    ◦ Nodes: SHA-256(0x01 || left_hash || right_hash).
• No Padding: Unlike generic implementations, the Phoenix system explicitly forbids padding the tree to a power of two, aligning with modern audit standards.
• Root Validation: The Merkle Root (e.g., 3aea...) serves as the "Sovereign Seal." The system validates the integrity of the entire project history by checking this single hash.
4. Operational Security & Evidence Sealing
SHA-256 is also deployed as a defensive weapon within the "Arsenal":
• Auth Hardening: The system uses crypto.subtle.digest("SHA-256", ...) to hash secrets before comparison. This enables Constant-Time Equality (ctEq) checks, preventing timing attacks on the Master Key.
• Evidence Sealing: During a "Nuclear Harvest," the system computes the SHA-256 hash of raw files ("Soul DNA") and ZIP archives (zip_sha256). This hash is written to the ledger as the blockid (idempotency key), ensuring that the evidence is immutable and cannot be uploaded twice.
• Identity Locking: Student profiles and "Tiger Scores" are bound to a composite hash ID (sha256(actor + commandid + payload_hash)), ensuring that identity and intent are permanently linked.
Discuss Merkle tree implementation.

Based on the Phoenix Flight archives, the Codex definitions, and the Omega Monolith architecture, the "Merkle Tree implementation" is not merely a data structure but the Cryptographic Constitution of the Empire. It enforces the "Ledger is Law" axiom by converting mutable database entries into an immutable, tamper-evident history.
Here is the detailed breakdown of the implementation:
1. The Core Algorithm: SHA-256 & Canonicalization
The implementation rejects standard JSON serialization in favor of deterministic enforcement to prevent "Hash Drift" (where the same data produces different hashes due to formatting).
• Algorithm: SHA-256 (specifically crypto.subtle.digest('SHA-256', ...) in the Cloudflare Worker runtime) [Source: 1887].
• Normalization Standard: RFC 8785 (JSON Canonicalization Scheme).
    ◦ The Problem: { "a": 1, "b": 2 } and { "b": 2, "a": 1 } are semantically identical but produce different SHA-256 hashes.
    ◦ The Fix: The canonicalJSON function forces lexicographic key sorting and specific number serialization before hashing. This ensures "Zero Drift" across different environments (Node.js vs. Cloudflare) [Source: 1841, 1888].
2. The Two-Tiered Integrity Structure
The system utilizes two distinct cryptographic structures depending on the data type:
A. The Ledger Spine (Linear Hash Chain)
For the Operational Ledger (Revenue, State Mutations), the system uses a Recursive Hash Chain. This ensures strict linear ordering (Time).
• Formula: Hash(n) = SHA-256( Canonical(Payload(n)) + Hash(n-1) )
• The Anchor: Every block contains a prev_hash field pointing to the block before it. Block 0 (Genesis) uses a null/zero hash.
• Implication: Changing a single byte in Block 10 changes its hash, which breaks the link to Block 11, invalidating the entire chain up to the current Head [Source: 1637, 1715].
B. The Codex/Audit Tree (CT-Style Merkle Tree)
For Static Assets (The 500-Step Map, Audit Packs), the system uses a Certificate Transparency (CT) Style Merkle Tree (RFC 6962). This allows for efficient inclusion proofs without re-hashing the whole chain.
• Leaf Hashing: Hash(Leaf) = SHA-256( 0x00 || Payload )
• Node Hashing: Hash(Node) = SHA-256( 0x01 || Left_Child_Hash || Right_Child_Hash )
• Domain Separation: The 0x00 and 0x01 prefixes prevent "Second Preimage Attacks" where a node could be spoofed as a leaf [Source: 9, 12, 18].
3. The "Deep Chain Fold" (Verification Protocol)
Verification is not passive; it is an active forensic process known as the Deep Chain Fold.
• Fast Seal (O(1)): In real-time, the system checks the verifier_state cursor against the current Ledger Head to ensure the last write didn't break the immediate link [Source: 1827].
• Full Seal (O(n)): Triggered via /verifychain, the system pulls every block from the D1 database, re-canonicalizes the payloads, and re-calculates the hashes from Genesis to Present.
    ◦ Verdict: If the calculated Root matches the stored Root, it returns OMEGA_SEAL_VERIFIED.
    ◦ Failure: If there is a mismatch, the system triggers the Fail-Closed Latch (setting halt_state = 1), locking the API (HTTP 423) until a Sovereign override [Source: 1827, 1892].
4. The "Immortal Stitch" (Concurrency Handling)
To handle high-velocity writes (the "Thundering Herd") without breaking the hash chain, the system uses a CAS-Retry Engine (Compare-And-Swap) inside the Durable Object.
1. Snapshot: Reads the current Head Hash.
2. Compute: Calculates the new Block Hash based on that snapshot.
3. Commit: Attempts to write the block only if the database Head hasn't moved.
4. Reflex: If the Head moved (drift), it recalculates and retries immediately (up to 3 times) rather than erroring out [Source: 1191, 1307].
5. Implementation Artifacts
The specific code enforcing this is located in the Phoenix Omega Monolith (phoenix_chat5_monolith.js / src/index.js):
// From Source 1888
const CRYPTO = {
    sha256: async (msg) => {
        const buf = await crypto.subtle.digest("SHA-256", new TextEncoder().encode(msg));
        return Array.from(new Uint8Array(buf)).map(b => b.toString(16).padStart(2, '0')).join('');
    },
    canon: (obj) => {
        if (obj === null || typeof obj !== "object") return JSON.stringify(obj);
        if (Array.isArray(obj)) return "[" + obj.map(CRYPTO.canon).join(",") + "]";
        return "{" + Object.keys(obj).sort().map(k => JSON.stringify(k) + ":" + CRYPTO.canon(obj[k])).join(",") + "}";
    }
};
Current Status: The Merkle Root is currently anchored at Ledger Height 62 (or 71 pending final sync), securing the "1 Billion VND" trajectory [Source: 1770, 1832].

Based on the Phoenix Flight archives—specifically the Codex, Vault, and Key files—here is the resolution matrix for the first 30 "Holes" (H0001–H0030). These answers represent the Canonical Truth enforced by the Phoenix Omega Monolith.
🦅 P01 Foundations of Truth (H0001–H0025)
• H0001 (Canonical Contract Artifact):
    ◦ Answer: phoenix/CANON.json.
    ◦ Context: This single JSON file is the "Immutable Law" defining authorized paths (api.write_path), canonical headers (api.auth_header), and the Gospel 444 visual palette. All scripts and Workers must read from this file; hardcoding is forbidden drift.
• H0002 (Secret Naming Convention):
    ◦ Answer: PHOENIX_MASTER_KEY.
    ◦ Context: The decision is sealed: Underscore-normalized (PHOENIX_MASTER_KEY) is the canonical truth. PHOENIXMASTERKEY is retained only as an alias during the 7-day migration window. DEATHSTAR_KEY is deprecated.
• H0003 (Truth Merge Rule):
    ◦ Answer: Ledger is Law.
    ◦ Context: If the code or documentation conflicts with the D1 Ledger, the Ledger prevails. Contradictions trigger a "Drift Report" or a "Fail-Closed" halt depending on severity.
• H0004 (Authoritative Version):
    ◦ Answer: Stored in wrangler.toml (PHOENIX_VERSION) and CANON.json.
    ◦ Context: It is surfaced via the /health endpoint and the X-Phoenix-Version response header.
• H0005 (Request ID Format):
    ◦ Answer: UUID v4.
    ◦ Context: A deterministic x-request-id is generated at the ingress (Worker) and propagated to all downstream components (Durable Objects, D1, Logging) to ensure full forensic traceability.
• H0006 (Allowed Open Endpoints):
    ◦ Answer: OPTIONS (CORS Preflight) and /health (Liveness).
    ◦ Context: OPTIONS must be unauthenticated to allow browser preflight checks. /health provides status without leaking data. All other endpoints require auth.
• H0007 (State-Changing Endpoints):
    ◦ Answer: /ledger/append (Canonical) or /execute (Legacy/Global).
    ◦ Context: Any endpoint that mutates state MUST be fail-closed, authenticated via x-deathstar-key, and logged to the ledger.
• H0008 (Payload Limits):
    ◦ Answer: 1MB.
    ◦ Context: A hard cap is enforced at the Worker level to prevent Denial of Service (DoS) or memory exhaustion in Durable Objects. Parse time is bound by the Worker's CPU limit.
• H0009 (Time Strategy):
    ◦ Answer: UTC (ISO 8601).
    ◦ Context: All timestamps are normalized to UTC strings (YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm:ss.sssZ) to ensure consistency across the distributed system.
• H0010 (Timestamp Collision Rule):
    ◦ Answer: Hybrid Logical Clock (HLC) or Monotonic Nonce.
    ◦ Context: If multiple events occur within the same millisecond, a logical counter (nonce) is incremented to strictly enforce ordering and prevent "Head Drift".
• H0011 (Runtime Parity):
    ◦ Answer: compatibility_date.
    ◦ Context: The compatibility_date in wrangler.toml (e.g., 2026-01-19) pins the Workers runtime version, ensuring tests run in Miniflare behave identically to production.
• H0012 (Fail-Closed Definition):
    ◦ Answer: Ambiguity = HALT.
    ◦ Context: Any failure—DB connection, JSON parse error, Auth mismatch, or Upstream timeout—triggers an immediate HTTP 4xx/5xx response and stops execution. The system never "guesses" or "degrades gracefully" into an insecure state.
• H0013 (Minimum Viable Evidence):
    ◦ Answer: report.json, manifest.json, run.log, and verify_pre/post.json.
    ◦ Context: A run is not "complete" until these artifacts are generated, zipped, hashed, and uploaded to R2.
• H0014 (Forbidden Data):
    ◦ Answer: Secrets and Unredacted PII.
    ◦ Context: Credentials (keys, tokens) and Personally Identifiable Information (names, faces) must NEVER enter the log stream or ledger payload. Redaction must occur before write.
• H0015 (Error Envelope Schema):
    ◦ Answer: { "ok": false, "error": "CODE", "message": "...", "request_id": "..." }.
    ◦ Context: A standardized JSON schema is enforced for all error responses to ensure clients can programmatically handle failures.
• H0016 (Canonical Error Codes):
    ◦ Answer: ERROR_CODES Enum.
    ◦ Context: Defined in src/errors.js, includes codes like CONFIG_MISSING_SECRET, ACCESS_DENIED, SCHEMA_VIOLATION.
• H0017 (Request ID Propagation):
    ◦ Answer: Mandatory Middleware.
    ◦ Context: A wrapper function ensures that x-request-id is attached to every response, including 401s and 500s generated by early exits.
• H0018 (Schema Evolution Plan):
    ◦ Answer: Additive Versioning (PHOENIX/1.3).
    ◦ Context: Changes are additive (new fields). Old fields are deprecated but not removed immediately. A schema_version header manages the transition.
• H0019 (Backwards Compatibility):
    ◦ Answer: Graceful Fallback / Dual Mode.
    ◦ Context: The system accepts both legacy and canonical formats for a defined window (e.g., 7 days) before enforcing a hard cut to the new schema.
• H0020 (Minimum Unit of Truth):
    ◦ Answer: One Row per User Action.
    ◦ Context: Each distinct user intent results in exactly one immutable ledger row. There is no "batching" that obscures individual actions.
• H0021 (Operation Correlation):
    ◦ Answer: correlation_id (or trace_id).
    ◦ Context: This field links multiple related ledger entries (e.g., a request and its resulting webhook) into a single logical operation.
• H0022 (Stop-Line Policy):
    ◦ Answer: Immediate HALT.
    ◦ Context: Any P0 invariant failure (e.g., broken hash chain, detected secret leak) triggers a "Stop-The-Line" event, halting all deployments until resolved by the Sovereign.
• H0023 (Artifact Naming):
    ◦ Answer: Content-Addressable (SHA-256).
    ◦ Context: Artifacts (zips, manifests) are named based on their SHA-256 hash to prevent collisions and ensure integrity.
• H0024 (Forbidden Code Patterns):
    ◦ Answer: eval() and new Function().
    ◦ Context: These are strictly banned by the Cloudflare Workers runtime and enforced via linter rules to prevent code injection.
• H0025 (Done/Sealed Declaration):
    ◦ Answer: EVIDENCE_SEALED Event.
    ◦ Context: A phase is only "Done" when an EVIDENCE_SEALED event containing the run's hash is successfully committed to the ledger.

🦅 P02 Auth & Identity (H0026–H0030)
• H0026 (Canonical Header):
    ◦ Answer: x-deathstar-key (Public) / x-sovereign-key (Internal).
    ◦ Context: x-deathstar-key is for external mutations. x-sovereign-key is for internal admin operations. This split is defined in CANON.json.
• H0027 (Auth for Metadata):
    ◦ Answer: YES.
    ◦ Context: Even metadata endpoints like /metrics or /status require authentication (unless explicitly public like /health) to prevent information leakage.
• H0028 (Bypass Prevention):
    ◦ Answer: Strict Path Matching & Normalization.
    ◦ Context: The router strictly matches paths and normalizes slashes (e.g., removing trailing slashes) before authentication checks to prevent bypass vulnerabilities.
• H0029 (Key Rotation Logic):
    ◦ Answer: Dual-Accept Window.
    ◦ Context: The system accepts both the old and new keys for a short period (e.g., 7 days) to allow for zero-downtime rotation. Usage is logged to track migration.
• H0030 (Missing Key Behavior):
    ◦ Answer: 401 Unauthorized.
    ◦ Context: Missing or invalid keys result in an immediate 401 (or 403). The system MUST NOT return 500 or any other error that could mask the security failure.

Based on the Phoenix Flight, Vault, Gem, and Codex archives, here is the resolution matrix for holes H0031–H0059, reflecting the Phoenix Omega Monolith (v55.2-SECURE / v100.0) configuration.
🦅 P02 Auth & Identity (H0031–H0050)
• H0031 (OPTIONS Preflight Security):
    ◦ Answer: Explicit Early Return (204 No Content).
    ◦ Context: The fetch handler checks if (req.method === "OPTIONS") as the very first step. It returns null body with CORS headers immediately, bypassing all auth checks and ledger logic to ensure browsers can probe safely without credentials [Source: 286, 292, 368, 399].
• H0032 (Origin Allowlist):
    ◦ Answer: env.ALLOWED_ORIGIN or * (with Warning).
    ◦ Context: To support the "Magic Chat" interface from various hosts, the system defaults to * but supports locking down via the ALLOWED_ORIGIN environment variable. If * is used, the system logs a CORS_ALLOW_ALL warning header [Source: 98, 105, 214, 382].
• H0033 (CORS Response Headers):
    ◦ Answer: Standardized Seal.
    ◦ Context: Every response includes:
        ▪ Access-Control-Allow-Origin: * (or specific origin)
        ▪ Access-Control-Allow-Methods: POST, GET, OPTIONS
        ▪ Access-Control-Allow-Headers: Content-Type, x-deathstar-key, x-sovereign-key, x-phoenix-nonce [Source: 188, 293, 297, 390].
• H0034 (Wildcard Origins):
    ◦ Answer: Allowed (PROD/DEV).
    ◦ Context: Explicitly allowed to enable the "Keyless Browser UI" pattern where the frontend might be hosted on different domains (Vercel, local, etc.) while the backend remains on Cloudflare [Source: 31, 382].
• H0035 (CSRF Protection):
    ◦ Answer: Custom Header Requirement.
    ◦ Context: The API enforces the presence of x-deathstar-key (or x-sovereign-key). Browsers do not send these custom headers automatically (unlike cookies), making standard CSRF attacks impossible. The UI is "Keyless" (Read-Only) or requires manual key entry for admin actions [Source: 330, 466].
• H0036 (Admin vs. Execute Key):
    ◦ Answer: Separation of Powers.
    ◦ Context:
        ▪ External/Mutation: Uses x-deathstar-key against PHOENIX_MASTER_KEY.
        ▪ Internal/Admin: Uses x-sovereign-key (typically for GOVERNOR operations).
        ▪ They are distinct credential paths [Source: 1080, 1176, 1182].
• H0037 (Auth Failure Throttling):
    ◦ Answer: RateLimiter Durable Object.
    ◦ Context: Auth failures trigger recordRequest(false), contributing to the per-IP or per-token bucket. Excessive failures return 429 RATE_LIMITED [Source: 197, 199, 215].
• H0038 (Auth Log Data):
    ◦ Answer: Redacted Metadata Only.
    ◦ Context: Logs capture request_id, status (e.g., 401), and error_code (e.g., ACCESS_DENIED). Secrets and raw header values are strictly forbidden in logs ("NO SECRETS IN CHAT mandate") [Source: 212, 245, 1146].
• H0039 (Header Formatting):
    ◦ Answer: Normalization & Trim.
    ◦ Context: The system handles case-insensitivity (name.toLowerCase()) and trims whitespace from header values before comparison [Source: 316, 1401].
• H0040 (Bearer vs. Header):
    ◦ Answer: Explicit Rejection / Drift.
    ◦ Context: Authorization: Bearer is identified as "Legacy Drift." The canonical implementation enforces x-deathstar-key. Requests using only Bearer tokens will fail with 401 [Source: 1176, 1192].
• H0041 (Internal Endpoint Policy):
    ◦ Answer: Strict Gate (except Health).
    ◦ Context: /health is public (returns status/version). /verifychain and /ledger/append are strictly gated behind x-deathstar-key or x-sovereign-key [Source: 1387, 1403, 1404].
• H0042 (Key Leakage Prevention):
    ◦ Answer: Headers & Architecture.
    ◦ Context: Referrer-Policy: no-referrer is set. The "Keyless Browser UI" architecture ensures the master key is never stored in the browser's local storage or sent via query params [Source: 317, 325, 806].
• H0043 (HTTPS Enforcement):
    ◦ Answer: HSTS Header.
    ◦ Context: Strict-Transport-Security: "max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains" is injected into every response [Source: 67, 317, 325].
• H0044 (Content-Type Validation):
    ◦ Answer: Strict application/json.
    ◦ Context: The json(req) helper throws INVALID_CONTENT_TYPE if the header is missing or incorrect, preventing parsing attacks [Source: 222, 230, 900].
• H0045 (Replay Protection):
    ◦ Answer: Nonce + Idempotency.
    ◦ Context: The system uses a Durable Object to track nonces. A duplicate nonce triggers a 409 Conflict. High-velocity inputs use CASRetry logic [Source: 1171, 1190, 1269].
• H0046 (Write Proof):
    ◦ Answer: EVIDENCE_SEALED & Chain Linkage.
    ◦ Context: An authenticated write returns a receipt with a hash. The Ledger appends an EVIDENCE_SEALED row referencing the artifact SHA, ensuring the write is auditable [Source: 1028, 1037].
• H0047 (Compromised Key Handling):
    ◦ Answer: Immediate Rotation via Wrangler.
    ◦ Context: The phoenix_repair_v8.ps1 script and wrangler secret put commands allow immediate rotation. The Worker supports a "Dual-Accept Window" (primary + secondary key) to rotate without downtime [Source: 330, 807, 1122].
• H0048 (Multi-tenant Keys):
    ◦ Answer: Shard by Risk/Actor.
    ◦ Context: The system supports per-actor sharding (idFromName('LEDGER_' + actorId)). It explicitly advises against sharding by user for everything, preferring "Risk Scope" [Source: 743, 1049, 1129].
• H0049 (Auth Negative Testing):
    ◦ Answer: Fail-Closed Assertion.
    ◦ Context: The AIDUT test suite specifically asserts that a missing or invalid key returns 401 or 403. If it returns 500 (crash) or 200 (leak), the deployment halts [Source: 1151, 1157, 1204].
• H0050 (Definitive Auth Contract):
    ◦ Answer: phoenix/CANON.json.
    ◦ Context: This file is the source of truth for auth.public_header, auth.internal_header, and auth.secret_name. It is verified by the scan_repo.sh script [Source: 1001, 1070, 1075].

🦅 P03 Input Validation & Runtime Bans (H0051–H0059)
• H0051 (Validation Library):
    ◦ Answer: Native / Zod (Target).
    ◦ Context: AJV caused crashes due to eval()/new Function() restrictions in Cloudflare Workers. The "Iron Clad" v55.1 kernel uses Native JS validation (manual if/else) to ensure stability. Future plans point to Zod for safe schema validation [Source: 185, 401, 519].
• H0052 (AJV Ban):
    ◦ Answer: Yes (Runtime Ban).
    ◦ Context: The environment explicitly forbids new Function, which AJV (standard build) relies on. Using it triggers Uncaught EvalError. The solution is using pre-compiled validators or a different library [Source: 51, 185].
• H0053 (JSON Limits):
    ◦ Answer: 1MB Cap.
    ◦ Context: MAX_BODY_BYTES = 1048576 (1MB). Requests larger than this are rejected with 413 PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE before parsing to prevent parser bombs [Source: 315, 899, 1251].
• H0054 (Unknown Fields):
    ◦ Answer: Strict Reject (v50+).
    ◦ Context: The "Iron Clad" and "Omega" specs define strict input rules (additionalProperties: false in schema intent). Unknown fields should trigger a validation error [Source: 62, 388, 422].
• H0055 (Array Validation):
    ◦ Answer: Schema Enforced.
    ◦ Context: Schemas define array types (e.g., items: { type: "string" }) and consistency checks [Source: 388].
• H0056 (Unicode Normalization):
    ◦ Answer: Canonical JSON.
    ◦ Context: The canonicalJSON function sorts keys and handles serialization (RFC 8785) to prevent hash drift, though explicit Unicode normalization (NFC/NFKC) code is not currently visible in the snippets [Source: 1477, 1506].
• H0057 (Numeric Fields):
    ◦ Answer: Regex Guard.
    ◦ Context: Critical numeric inputs (like ledger height) are validated with regex ^+$ before arithmetic to prevent coercion attacks [Source: 1069, 1159].
• H0058 (Enum Validation):
    ◦ Answer: Central Registry.
    ◦ Context: ERROR_CODES and other enums (Status, Tier) are defined in central registries and validated against allowed values [Source: 266, 314, 624].
• H0059 (Schema Versioning):
    ◦ Answer: x-phoenix-schema-version Header.
    ◦ Context: This header dictates which schema the payload must adhere to (e.g., PHOENIX/1.3). Mismatches are rejected [Source: 314, 324].

Based on the Phoenix Flight, Vault, and Codex archives, here is the resolution matrix for holes H0060–H0090, reflecting the Phoenix Omega Monolith (v55.2-SECURE / v100.0) configuration.
🦅 P03 Input Validation & Runtime Bans (H0060–H0075)
• H0060 (Validation Error Response):
    ◦ Answer: Standardized Envelope (INVALID_SCHEMA).
    ◦ Context: Validation failures return HTTP 400 with: { "ok": false, "error": "INVALID_SCHEMA", "details": [ "field 'actor' is required" ] }. This is enforced by the responder.err helper [Source: 348, 1457].
• H0061 (Content-Length Validation):
    ◦ Answer: 1MB Hard Cap.
    ◦ Context: MAX_BODY_BYTES = 1048576 (1MB). If Content-Length exceeds this, or if the stream grows larger during read, the Worker aborts with 413 PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE [Source: 348, 1457].
• H0062 (JSON Injection Prevention):
    ◦ Answer: Sanitization & Redaction.
    ◦ Context: All logs pass through a central logger that redacts secrets (redact_json). Admin UI renders data using text-only nodes or sanitized HTML (Gospel 444 compliance) to prevent XSS/Injection [Source: 1386, 1457].
• H0063 (Prototype Pollution):
    ◦ Answer: Object.freeze & Native Validation.
    ◦ Context: The "Iron Clad" validation uses native JS checks (no vulnerable libraries). Key objects like LAW and CONFIG are Object.freeze()d immediately upon declaration [Source: 1458, 1428].
• H0064 (Multipart Support):
    ◦ Answer: Explicit Rejection.
    ◦ Context: The API enforces Content-Type: application/json. Requests with multipart/form-data are rejected with 415 UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA_TYPE [Source: 300, 1458].
• H0065 (Large String Policy):
    ◦ Answer: Truncation & Cap.
    ◦ Context: String fields (like payload.raw_verbatim) are capped (e.g., 64KB). Extremely large strings trigger a validation error rather than truncation to avoid data corruption (Fail-Closed) [Source: 348, 1458].
• H0066 (Timestamp Validation):
    ◦ Answer: Server-Side Authority (HLC).
    ◦ Context: Client timestamps (ts) are compared against server Date.now(). If drift exceeds 2000ms, the request is rejected (HALT::TIME_DRIFT_DETECTED). The ledger uses Hybrid Logical Clocks (HLC) for ordering [Source: 687, 1428].
• H0067 (Actor Identity Validation):
    ◦ Answer: Header Binding.
    ◦ Context: The actor_id in the payload MUST match the identity associated with the x-deathstar-key provided. Mismatches trigger ACCESS_DENIED [Source: 57, 1458].
• H0068 (Checksum Computation):
    ◦ Answer: Server-Side Mandatory.
    ◦ Context: While clients may send a checksum, the server always re-computes SHA-256(canonical(payload)) to verify integrity. Client claims are ignored for security [Source: 1130, 1459].
• H0069 (Key Ordering):
    ◦ Answer: RFC 8785 Canonicalization.
    ◦ Context: Before hashing, the canonicalJSON function sorts object keys lexicographically. This ensures {"a":1, "b":2} and {"b":2, "a":1} produce the same hash [Source: 748, 1459].
• H0070 (Partial Validation):
    ◦ Answer: Fail-Closed (All or Nothing).
    ◦ Context: "There is no partial success." If one item in a batch is invalid, the entire batch is rejected. This prevents partial ledger states [Source: 95, 1459].
• H0071 (Dry Run Logic):
    ◦ Answer: Simulated Response.
    ◦ Context: If dry_run: true is passed, the Worker validates the schema and auth, calculates the hash, but skips the D1 INSERT. It returns {"status": "DRY_RUN_OK"} [Source: 87, 1459].
• H0072 (Dry Run Bypass):
    ◦ Answer: Mutation Gate.
    ◦ Context: The write logic is encapsulated in appendLedgerEntry. The dry_run check happens inside this atomic function, preventing bypass via alternate routes [Source: 1459].
• H0073 (Response Scrubbing):
    ◦ Answer: Redaction Scan.
    ◦ Context: A final pass checks the response body for patterns matching known secrets (e.g., sk-..., PHOENIX_MASTER_KEY). If found, it halts or redacts before sending [Source: 1260, 1460].
• H0074 (Error Stability):
    ◦ Answer: Enum-Only Leakage.
    ◦ Context: Errors return stable codes (e.g., SCHEMA_VIOLATION) from the ERROR_CODES enum. Internal stack traces are suppressed in production [Source: 266, 1460].
• H0075 (Unit Tests):
    ◦ Answer: AIDUT & Chaos-07.
    ◦ Context: The PHOENIX_LOADTEST_AUDIT_FINAL.sh (AIDUT) suite performs negative tests (auth fail, bad schema) against the live Worker to prove these controls exist [Source: 1209, 1460].

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
🦅 P04 Ledger Integrity & Hashchain (H0076–H0090)
• H0076 (Hashchain Algorithm):
    ◦ Answer: SHA-256 Recursive.
    ◦ Context: Hash(N) = SHA-256( Prev_Hash || Canonical(Payload) || Nonce ). This binds position, content, and history [Source: 748, 1460].
• H0077 (Deleted Entries):
    ◦ Answer: Append-Only (Soft Delete).
    ◦ Context: "Ledger is Law." Rows are never DELETEd. A new row with type: "DELETION" is appended, invalidating the previous state logically but preserving the cryptographic chain [Source: 65, 1461].
• H0078 (Chain Partitioning):
    ◦ Answer: Sharded by Actor.
    ◦ Context: To solve Hole #5 (Shard-Freeze), chains are partitioned per-actor (idFromName('LEDGER_' + actorId)). This allows parallel scaling while keeping individual histories linear [Source: 1190, 1461].
• H0079 (Linkage Enforcement):
    ◦ Answer: Durable Object Lock.
    ◦ Context: The DO enforces prev_hash checking. It holds the head_hash in memory. If an incoming write claims a different prev_hash, it is rejected (Drift) [Source: 1193, 1461].
• H0080 (Fork Detection):
    ◦ Answer: CASRetry (Optimistic Locking).
    ◦ Context: If two requests try to write to block N+1 simultaneously, the database rejects the second one (Duplicate Key/Nonce). The CASRetry engine then catches this, reads the new head, and retries as block N+2 [Source: 1190, 1461].
• H0081 (Reorg Policy):
    ◦ Answer: Bounded Retry / Halt.
    ◦ Context: If a write fails mid-flight, the CASRetry loop attempts 3 times. If it fails 3 times, it returns HEAD_DRIFT_EXHAUSTED (503). No "gaps" are recorded; the transaction simply fails [Source: 1190, 1461].
• H0082 (Verifychain Output):
    ◦ Answer: { ok: true, height: 62, hash: "..." }.
    ◦ Context: The /verifychain endpoint re-computes the entire hash chain from Genesis. It returns the current validated height and root hash [Source: 1123, 1462].
• H0083 (Verifychain DOS):
    ◦ Answer: Fast Seal vs. Full Audit.
    ◦ Context: Real-time checks use "Fast Seal" (O(1) check of Head). "Full Audit" (O(n)) is a heavy operation restricted to Admin/internal calls or scheduled tasks [Source: 830, 1462].
• H0084 (Root Signing):
    ◦ Answer: Raw SHA-256.
    ◦ Context: The chain uses raw SHA-256 hashes. Digital signatures (Ed25519) are used for payload verification (H-01), but the chain linkage itself is hash-based [Source: 1130, 1462].
• H0085 (Root Storage):
    ◦ Answer: D1 Blocks Table.
    ◦ Context: The "Truth" lives in the blocks table in D1. R2 serves as a "Cold Journal" backup [Source: 1274, 1462].
• H0086 (Out-of-Order Handling):
    ◦ Answer: HLC (Hybrid Logical Clock).
    ◦ Context: To solve Hole #6, the system uses HLCs. Events are ordered by {physical_time, logical_counter} to resolve sub-millisecond collisions deterministically [Source: 1193, 1462].
• H0087 (Created_At Source):
    ◦ Answer: Server-Side Only.
    ◦ Context: The timestamp field in the block is generated by the Durable Object at the moment of serialization. Client timestamps are stored in metadata but are not authoritative for ordering [Source: 687, 1463].
• H0088 (Request ID Storage):
    ◦ Answer: Yes (Audit Field).
    ◦ Context: request_id (UUID) is a mandatory column/field in the ledger to trace every committed block back to its ingress request [Source: 317, 1463].
• H0089 (Actor Schema):
    ◦ Answer: Enum-Validated.
    ◦ Context: actor_id must match a known entity (e.g., AGENT-100, AGENT-99, STUDENT). Random strings are rejected [Source: 57, 1463].
• H0090 (Dedupe Policy):
    ◦ Answer: Idempotency Key (409).
    ◦ Context: Every write requires an idempotency_key (derived from payload hash). If a key exists within the 24h window, the server returns 409 Conflict (or cached success) to prevent bloat [Source: 1269, 1463].

Based on the comprehensive archives (PhoenixFlight series, VaultB, and Gem), here is the resolution matrix for holes H0091–H0119.
These answers reflect the Phoenix Omega Monolith (v47.2-ULTIMA) configuration, specifically the "Immortal Stitch" logic and the "Shard-Freeze" patch.
🦅 P04 Ingest & Integrity (H0091–H0100)
• H0091 (Idempotency Scope):
    ◦ Answer: Global command_id Scope.
    ◦ Context: The idempotency table in D1 enforces uniqueness on command_id. This key acts as the global transaction handle. Reusing a command_id across different endpoints or days will return the cached result of the first successful execution [Source: 1296, 1341, 1435].
• H0092 (Replay Window):
    ◦ Answer: 24 Hours (TTL).
    ◦ Context: The idempotency table entries have an expires_at timestamp. Source 1285 and 1301 explicitly confirm a "24-hour TTL" to prevent database bloat while covering the "Thundering Herd" retry window.
• H0093 (D1 Write Success / Response Fail):
    ◦ Answer: Client Retry -> Idempotent Success (200 OK).
    ◦ Context: If the Worker crashes after writing to D1 but before responding, the client retries with the same command_id. The DO sees the ID in the idempotency table and returns the stored receipt (status: ANCHORED) without re-running the logic [Source: 1190, 1284, 1440].
• H0094 (DO State vs. D1 Fail):
    ◦ Answer: Atomic Transaction (blockConcurrencyWhile).
    ◦ Context: The system uses this.state.blockConcurrencyWhile to wrap the D1 write. If D1 fails, the entire transaction rolls back. The DO does not update its internal state if the persistent storage fails. If the state desynchronizes, the DO executes a "Panic Halt" [Source: 566, 799, 1278].
• H0095 (Ledger Invariants Checker):
    ◦ Answer: H-907 Vigilance / Drone 03.
    ◦ Context: "Drone 03 (The Analyst)" and the "Integrity Scan Drone" run continuous verifychain audits (O(n) Merkle Fold) to detect "Nonce Gaps" or hash mismatches. If drift is found, the system enters INCIDENT-2 (FREEZE) [Source: 909, 1335, 1604].
• H0096 (Admin Export Proof):
    ◦ Answer: NDJSON with Hash Links.
    ◦ Context: The export handler streams NDJSON where every row includes prev_hash. External auditors can re-hash the stream to prove immutability without access to the live DB [Source: 847, 896].
• H0097 (Schema Migrations vs. Hashing):
    ◦ Answer: Canonical JSON (RFC 8785).
    ◦ Context: The system sorts keys lexicographically before hashing. New columns (additive changes) are handled by versioning the schema header (x-phoenix-schema-version). Old rows retain their original hash based on the payload at the time of write [Source: 856, 1496].
• H0098 (Redaction Strategy):
    ◦ Answer: Pre-Write Scrub (Face-Safe).
    ◦ Context: Redaction happens before the ledger write. The "Evidence Ledger" stores the scrubbed transcript. The "Operational Ledger" stores only the hash. Raw unredacted data (if any) is ephemeral and never hits D1 [Source: 1276, 1287].
• H0099 (Completeness Evidence):
    ◦ Answer: Monotonic Nonce Continuity.
    ◦ Context: The system verifies that block[n].nonce == block[n-1].nonce + 1. Any gap (e.g., Nonce 3 missing) triggers an immediate system HALT until resolved by Agent-100 [Source: 910, 1250].
• H0100 (Sealing Process):
    ◦ Answer: Omega Seal (Event h62_seal).
    ◦ Context: Sealing is an explicit event type. The system writes a special block containing the Merkle Root of the entire chain up to that height. This "checkpoints" the history [Source: 885, 891].

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
🦅 P05 Cloudflare DO Concurrency & State (H0101–H0119)
• H0101 (Single Writer Rule):
    ◦ Answer: Enforced via PhoenixLedgerDO.
    ◦ Context: Direct D1 writes from the Worker are forbidden. All state-changing paths MUST route through stub.fetch(req), ensuring the Durable Object acts as the serialized "Sovereign Authority" [Source: 586, 799, 1450].
• H0102 (DO Storage Schema):
    ◦ Answer: SQLite (D1) + KV.
    ◦ Context: The "Zenith" engine moved from DO native storage (state.storage) to D1 (state.storage.sql) for complex querying. Versioning is handled via the new_sqlite_classes migration tag in wrangler.toml [Source: 771, 1111].
• H0103 (DO Alarm Usage):
    ◦ Answer: Heartbeat & Consensus Heal.
    ◦ Context: Alarms are used for sub-minute maintenance tasks that Cron Triggers can't handle. Failure mode: If alarms fail >4 times, the system sets halt_state = 1 [Source: 771, 775].
• H0104 (Deadlock Prevention):
    ◦ Answer: Timeouts & Shard Locking.
    ◦ Context: The blockConcurrencyWhile lock prevents race conditions but risks deadlocks if D1 hangs. The "Global Timeout Guard" (30s) kills the request if it hangs, freeing the DO [Source: 217, 775].
• H0105 (DO Exceptions):
    ◦ Answer: Panic & Halt (Fail-Closed).
    ◦ Context: Uncaught exceptions in the DO trigger a restart of the object. Critical logic errors trigger the "Halt Latch" (halted = 1), returning 423 LOCKED to all future requests until manual intervention [Source: 854, 1241].
• H0106 (DO Re-instantiation Safety):
    ◦ Answer: Stateless RAM / Durable SQL.
    ◦ Context: The system treats in-memory variables as volatile caches. On re-instantiation (cold start), it rehydrates critical state (like the Head Hash) from the D1 blocks table before accepting writes [Source: 1199, 1200].
• H0107 (Endpoint Protection):
    ◦ Answer: Binding-Only Access.
    ◦ Context: DO endpoints are not exposed to the public internet. They can only be called by a Worker holding the GOVERNOR_DO binding [Source: 1340, 1341].
• H0108 (DO Rate Limiting):
    ◦ Answer: Per-IP / Per-Token via RateLimiter DO.
    ◦ Context: A dedicated RateLimiter DO tracks token buckets. Sovereign keys bypass this limit (RPSLIMIT override) [Source: 147, 1237].
• H0109 (Object Naming - Sharding):
    ◦ Answer: Per-Actor (LEDGER_${actorId}).
    ◦ Context: To fix "Hole #5" (Shard Freeze), the system moved from a Singleton (global) to per-actor sharding. This prevents one heavy tenant from locking the entire system [Source: 1250, 1252, 1340].
• H0110 (Deterministic Testing):
    ◦ Answer: Miniflare via compatibility_date.
    ◦ Context: The wrangler.toml pins the compatibility_date (2026-01-19) to ensure the local Miniflare environment matches the production V8 runtime exactly [Source: 1021, 1361].
• H0111 (Storage Growth Policy):
    ◦ Answer: "The Void" (Data Purge).
    ◦ Context: Automated scripts hard-delete "Evidence Ledger" (raw audio/transcripts) older than 90 days. "Operational Ledger" (financials) is kept forever [Source: 1409].
• H0112 (Atomicity Contract):
    ◦ Answer: SQL Transaction Wrapper.
    ◦ Context: The PhoenixLedgerDO uses state.storage.sql.exec within a transaction. It returns 200 OK only if the SQL commit succeeds. Any SQL error throws an exception, returning 500 [Source: 773, 1134].
• H0113 (Internal Steps Logging):
    ◦ Answer: Payload Fingerprinting.
    ◦ Context: The system logs the SHA-256 hash of payloads and internal steps, but never the raw secrets or PII, ensuring the "No Secrets in Chat" mandate is respected [Source: 1184].
• H0114 (DO Health Check):
    ◦ Answer: Proxied /health.
    ◦ Context: The Worker's /health endpoint calls the DO's handleHealth() method. If the DO is down or overloaded, the health check fails, signaling the orchestrator [Source: 690, 896].
• H0115 (Migration Safety):
    ◦ Answer: Shadow Updates.
    ◦ Context: New DO code is deployed to a "Shadow" worker first. The system compares the behavior of the old and new versions before switching traffic (Blue/Green) [Source: 577].
• H0116 (Prevent Double Processing):
    ◦ Answer: Idempotency Cache Check.
    ◦ Context: The very first step in the DO's write handler is SELECT 1 FROM idempotency WHERE command_id = ?. If found, it returns the stored result immediately [Source: 1284, 1394].
• H0117 (DO Timeout Budget):
    ◦ Answer: 30 Seconds (Hard).
    ◦ Context: Enforced by the Worker using Promise.race against an AbortController. If the DO takes >30s, the Worker cuts the connection and returns 504 [Source: 217, 1367].
• H0118 (Batch Writes):
    ◦ Answer: Atomic Single Transaction.
    ◦ Context: Batch writes are wrapped in a single SQL transaction. Either all items in the batch are committed, or none are. This prevents "partial batch" corruption [Source: 1546].
• H0119 (Monotonic Sequence):
    ◦ Answer: D1 AUTOINCREMENT.
    ◦ Context: The ledger table uses INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT for the id and strict nonce sequencing in the blocks table to guarantee gap detection [Source: 1285, 1302].

Based on the Phoenix Flight, Vault, Gem, and Codex archives (specifically Sources 1190–1463), here is the resolution matrix for holes H0120–H0150.
These answers reflect the Phoenix Omega Monolith (v47.2-ULTIMA) configuration, specifically the Hole #5 (Shard-Freeze) and Hole #3 (Journal-Fail) patches.
🦅 P05 Cloudflare DO Concurrency & State (H0120–H0125)
• H0120 (DO vs D1 Ordering):
    ◦ Answer: Atomic Lock (blockConcurrencyWhile).
    ◦ Context: The Durable Object acts as the "Serializer." It does not update its internal this.state (memory) until the D1 transaction (INSERT) returns success. If D1 fails, the DO state rolls back, ensuring the "Head" never drifts from the database [Source: 799, 1278].
• H0121 (DO Success / D1 Fail):
    ◦ Answer: Impossible by Design.
    ◦ Context: The write logic is wrapped in await this.state.blockConcurrencyWhile(async () => { ... }). The D1 write happens inside this block. If it throws, the block exits with an error, and the DO's in-memory state is not advanced. There is no "success" if D1 fails [Source: 1193, 1411].
• H0122 (Verifychain Starvation):
    ◦ Answer: Fast Seal (O(1)) vs. Full Audit (O(n)).
    ◦ Context: Routine ingest uses a "Fast Seal" (checking only the head hash). The "Full Chain Fold" (verifychain) is a heavy operation restricted to admin/maintenance windows or specific drones to prevent locking the DO for writers [Source: 830, 902].
• H0123 (Multi-region Consistency):
    ◦ Answer: Global Singleton (Durable Object).
    ◦ Context: Cloudflare guarantees that a specific Durable Object instance (e.g., LEDGER_AGENT_100) exists in only one location at a time. All requests worldwide route to that single instance, guaranteeing strong consistency without conflict [Source: 802, 1411].
• H0124 (DO Exception Logging):
    ◦ Answer: Structured Panic.
    ◦ Context: Exceptions in the DO are caught, wrapped in a standard error envelope ({ok: false, error: "DO_PANIC", request_id: ...}), and logged to the console (which flows to R2/Logpush) before the request is rejected [Source: 1184, 1271].
• H0125 (DO Stop-Line Triggers):
    ◦ Answer: Halt Latch (Three Conditions).
    ◦ Context: The system auto-halts (sets halt_state=1) if:
        1. Chain Rupture: block.parent_hash !== prevHash.
        2. Shard Drift: env.SHARD_KEY !== db.shard_lock (Hole #5).
        3. Alarm Loop: Internal alarms fail > 3 times consecutively [Source: 875, 1271].

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
🦅 P06 D1 Database & Query Safety (H0126–H0150)
• H0126 (D1 Schema Definition):
    ◦ Answer: schema.sql / DO Boot Script.
    ◦ Context: The schema is defined in code (PhoenixLedgerDO constructor) using CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS. While wrangler d1 migrations is supported for updates, the Monolith enforces the baseline schema at startup to ensure self-healing [Source: 1411, 1412].
• H0127 (Create Table Race):
    ◦ Answer: Boot-Time Only.
    ◦ Context: CREATE TABLE statements are executed only during the Durable Object's initialization phase (constructor), which is single-threaded per ID. This prevents race conditions where two requests try to create tables simultaneously [Source: 1411].
• H0128 (Migration Order):
    ◦ Answer: Wrangler Sequencing.
    ◦ Context: D1 migrations are strictly ordered by numeric prefix (e.g., 0001_init.sql, 0002_add_index.sql) enforced by the wrangler CLI during deployment [Source: 1002].
• H0129 (Max Row Cap):
    ◦ Answer: LIMIT 50 (Default) / 100 (Max).
    ◦ Context: All SELECT queries (Admin/API) must include a LIMIT clause. The architecture documentation specifies hard caps to prevent memory exhaustion [Source: 128, 997].
• H0130 (Injection Prevention):
    ◦ Answer: Parameter Binding (?).
    ◦ Context: The code explicitly uses stmt.bind(val1, val2) for all user inputs. String concatenation for SQL is strictly forbidden and flagged by the "AcornGuard" scanner [Source: 1107, 1124].
• H0131 (Large Export Strategy):
    ◦ Answer: NDJSON Streaming.
    ◦ Context: Exports are streamed line-by-line (Newline Delimited JSON) to avoid buffering the entire dataset in Worker memory. This allows exporting gigabytes of data within CPU limits [Source: 847, 896].
• H0132 (Indexing Strategy):
    ◦ Answer: Query-Driven.
    ◦ Context: Indexes are strictly defined on keys used for filtering: student_id, timestamp, type, and actor. Arbitrary column indexing is avoided to minimize write overhead [Source: 64, 1449].
• H0133 (Pagination Stability):
    ◦ Answer: Implicit Cursor (ID/Time).
    ◦ Context: While "Offset/Limit" is supported for simple UI, stable pagination relies on WHERE id < last_seen_id ORDER BY id DESC to handle real-time insertions without skipped rows [Source: 128, 159].
• H0134 (JSON Storage):
    ◦ Answer: TEXT Column.
    ◦ Context: JSON payloads are stored as TEXT. D1 (SQLite) supports JSON functions (json_extract), enabling safe querying of fields without schema changes [Source: 1411].
• H0135 (Retention Strategy):
    ◦ Answer: Two-Tier (Ledger vs. Evidence).
    ◦ Context:
        ▪ Operational Ledger (Financials): Kept Forever ("Ledger is Law").
        ▪ Evidence Ledger (Raw Audio): Purged after 90 days via automated cron script to save costs [Source: 123, 1276].
• H0136 (Prevent Full Table Scan):
    ◦ Answer: Mandatory LIMIT.
    ◦ Context: The internal query builder enforces a default LIMIT if none is provided by the client, preventing accidental SELECT * FROM ledger [Source: 152].
• H0137 (Transient Failure Retry):
    ◦ Answer: CAS-Retry (Immortal Stitch).
    ◦ Context: If a D1 write fails due to locking or contention (SQLITE_BUSY), the CASRetry loop waits (backoff) and retries up to 3 times before failing closed [Source: 1190, 1276].
• H0138 (Connection Check):
    ◦ Answer: Startup Probe.
    ◦ Context: The deployment script runs SELECT 1 AS ok against the D1 binding to verify connectivity before routing traffic [Source: 997, 1002].
• H0139 (Audit Columns):
    ◦ Answer: NOT NULL Constraint.
    ◦ Context: The blocks table schema defines actor, timestamp, and hash as NOT NULL, rejecting any write that attempts to omit them [Source: 1411].
• H0140 (Schema Versioning per Row):
    ◦ Answer: Header + Payload.
    ◦ Context: The x-phoenix-schema-version header tracks the schema version at ingest time. The payload stores the version used, allowing the reader to handle schema evolution [Source: 1496].
• H0141 (Idempotency Table):
    ◦ Answer: Yes (idempotency).
    ◦ Context: A dedicated table CREATE TABLE idempotency (command_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY...) stores response hashes to prevent replay attacks [Source: 1187, 1270].
• H0142 (Deletions as Events):
    ◦ Answer: Soft Deletes Only.
    ◦ Context: Physical DELETE is forbidden on the ledger. Deletions are recorded as new events (type: "DELETION") that mark previous entries as void [Source: 1461].
• H0143 (Hot Partition Prevention):
    ◦ Answer: Per-Actor Sharding.
    ◦ Context: Hole #5 was patched by sharding the Durable Object by actor_id (e.g., LEDGER_STUDENT_01). This prevents one heavy user from degrading performance for others [Source: 1383, 1412].
• H0144 (Backfill Strategy):
    ◦ Answer: Migration Scripts.
    ◦ Context: Adding indexes or columns requires a migration script. Backfilling data is handled by a background "Drone" task that reads/updates rows in batches [Source: 1190].
• H0145 (Audit Query):
    ◦ Answer: /verifychain.
    ◦ Context: The system exposes a /verifychain endpoint that runs a checksum validation (Merkle fold) over the D1 table to detect anomalies like missing nonces or hash mismatches [Source: 844, 1335].
• H0146 (Export Ordering):
    ◦ Answer: Monotonic ID.
    ◦ Context: Exports are always ORDER BY height ASC (or id ASC). Since id is AUTOINCREMENT, this guarantees strict chronological order [Source: 122, 1285].
• H0147 (Large Payload Storage):
    ◦ Answer: Hybrid (Pointer).
    ◦ Context: "Evidence Ledger" (Heavy) goes to R2. "Operational Ledger" (Light) stores the R2 key/hash. D1 never stores blobs > 1MB [Source: 1276].
• H0148 (Query Param Safety):
    ◦ Answer: Validation & Binding.
    ◦ Context: Query parameters are validated against allowed types (int, string, enum) and bound as parameters, never interpolated [Source: 1501].
• H0149 (Performance Budget):
    ◦ Answer: Dashboard Monitoring.
    ◦ Context: Queries exceeding the budget (e.g., >50ms execution) are flagged in the logs/dashboard for optimization [Source: 170].
• H0150 (Disaster Recovery):
    ◦ Answer: Journal Replay.
    ◦ Context: If D1 is corrupted, the "Journal Guard" allows rebuilding the state from the R2 backups/manifests (Hole #3 fix) [Source: 1224, 1383].

Based on the Phoenix Flight, Gem, GPT, and Vault archives, here is the resolution matrix for holes H0151–H0179, reflecting the Phoenix Omega Monolith (Stage 4 RC3) configuration.
🦅 P07 R2 Artifacts, Evidence Sealing, and Upload Semantics (H0151–H0175)
• H0151 (Artifact Schema):
    ◦ Answer: RUN_MANIFEST.json (Canonical).
    ◦ Context: The manifest strictly defines the run environment: { "run_label", "env", "base_url", "artifacts": { "health_before": {sha, file}, ... } }. It creates a cryptographic binding between the code execution and the resulting evidence files [Source: 885, 1003, 1202].
• H0152 (Zip SHA Integrity):
    ◦ Answer: Post-Creation Calculation.
    ◦ Context: The SHA-256 hash is computed only after the zip command successfully exits. zip_sha="$(sha_file "$zip_path")". This ensures the hash represents the exact bytes sitting on the disk [Source: 865, 1201].
• H0153 (Zip Recursion Prevention):
    ◦ Answer: External Build Directory.
    ◦ Context: Fixed in RC2/RC3. The script creates a temporary directory (zip_build_dir=$(mktemp -d)) outside the report directory to generate the zip file, preventing the zip -r . command from recursively swallowing its own output [Source: 565, 865, 1246].
• H0154 (Wrangler Syntax Drift):
    ◦ Answer: Auto-Detect with Retry.
    ◦ Context: The r2_put function attempts wrangler r2 object put bucket key (Style A). If that fails, it retries with bucket/key (Style B). RC3 improves this by retrying Style A twice to rule out transient network errors before falling back [Source: 575, 861, 1016].
• H0155 (Upload Idempotency):
    ◦ Answer: Ledger BlockID = EVIDENCE_${zip_sha}.
    ◦ Context: The ledger commit uses the Zip SHA as the idempotency key. If the upload is repeated (e.g., script re-run), the ledger rejects the write with 409 Conflict (or returns cached success), preventing duplicate evidence entries [Source: 561, 1021].
• H0156 (Upload Verification):
    ◦ Answer: Download & unzip -t.
    ◦ Context: Immediately after upload, the script downloads the object from R2 to a temp file and runs unzip -t. If this fails, the pipeline HALTS, ensuring no corrupt evidence is ever sealed in the ledger [Source: 866, 1030].
• H0157 (R2 Key Naming):
    ◦ Answer: Content-Addressable (${sha}.zip).
    ◦ Context: Keys are named ${EVIDENCE_R2_PREFIX}${zip_sha}.zip. This guarantees that identical content always maps to the same key, enforcing data deduplication at the storage layer [Source: 830, 1194].
• H0158 (Retention Policy):
    ◦ Answer: "The Void" (90 Days).
    ◦ Context: While the Operational Ledger (money) is kept forever, the Evidence Ledger (raw audio/logs in R2) is subject to a 90-day purge cycle via automated scripts to manage storage costs [Source: 537, 801, 1409].
• H0159 (Environment Metadata):
    ◦ Answer: Captured in Manifest.
    ◦ Context: The manifest explicitly captures wrangler_version, worker_git_sha, node_version, and host_uname. This creates a forensic snapshot of the toolchain used to deploy [Source: 851, 885, 1266].
• H0160 (Negative Evidence):
    ◦ Answer: Error Staging directory.
    ◦ Context: stage_error() copies payload and response bodies of failed requests to evidence/errors/. The RC2 script specifically logs auth-negative tests (AUTH_NEG_EXPECT_CODES) [Source: 1045, 1053].
• H0161 (Secret Prevention in Zip):
    ◦ Answer: Redaction Scan Gate.
    ◦ Context: redaction_scan() runs grep against the report directory for known secret patterns (hex keys, "Bearer", etc.) before zipping. If SAFE_EVIDENCE=1, any hit triggers an immediate HALT [Source: 567, 894, 1206].
• H0162 (Creation Evidence Chain):
    ◦ Answer: Ledger Linkage.
    ◦ Context: The EVIDENCE_SEALED ledger event acts as the timestamped proof of creation, linking the Actor (via API Key) to the R2 Object (via Hash) [Source: 1002, 1188].
• H0163 (Partial Upload Failures):
    ◦ Answer: Atomic Halt.
    ◦ Context: wrangler r2 object put is treated as atomic. If the command returns non-zero, the script HALTS. There is no "resume," only "retry from start" (which works due to idempotency) [Source: 861, 1195].
• H0164 (Max Artifact Size):
    ◦ Answer: Implicit Wrangler Limits.
    ◦ Context: While S0089 defines a payload cap for ingest, the shell script relies on Wrangler's internal upload limits. H0164 is currently an "Amber" gap in the shell script itself (no explicit du check) [Source: 1204].
• H0165 (Log Policy):
    ◦ Answer: Full Capture, Redacted.
    ◦ Context: RUN_STDOUT and RUN_STDERR are captured to files and included in the zip. The redaction scanner ensures no secrets leak within them [Source: 823, 1190].
• H0166 (Creation Timing):
    ◦ Answer: Post-Verify Gate.
    ◦ Context: Evidence is sealed only after harvest_runner completes and /verifychain returns success. You cannot seal evidence of a failed run (Fail-Closed) [Source: 1203].
• H0167 (Ledger Record Integrity):
    ◦ Answer: Payload Hash Match.
    ◦ Context: The ledger entry payload includes { evidence: { zip_sha256: ... } }. This allows any auditor to download the R2 file, hash it, and match it against the immutable ledger record [Source: 1003].
• H0168 (R2 Down Fallback):
    ◦ Answer: Stop-The-Line (HALT).
    ◦ Context: If R2 is unreachable, the script halts with R2 upload failed. It does not fallback to local-only, ensuring the "Rule of Two" (Ledger + Journal) is never violated [Source: 861, 1187].
• H0169 (Bucket Targeting):
    ◦ Answer: Env-Bound Verification.
    ◦ Context: The script explicitly checks wrangler r2 object head "$R2_BUCKET_NAME" using the passed WRANGLER_ENV. This prevents staging artifacts from landing in prod buckets [Source: 861, 1195].
• H0170 (Manifest Signing):
    ◦ Answer: Hash Chain (SHA-256).
    ◦ Context: While RSA-PSS-256 is mentioned for archival capsules (H-01), the Stage 4 script currently relies on the Ledger's internal hash chain to "sign" the manifest's existence in time [Source: 689, 885].
• H0171 (Tool Drift):
    ◦ Answer: Version Capture.
    ◦ Context: wrangler --version is written to wrangler_version.txt inside the evidence pack to debug syntax drift later [Source: 885, 1266].
• H0172 (HTTP Response Capture):
    ◦ Answer: http_json_strict.
    ◦ Context: This helper function captures both the HTTP Status Code and the Body. It halts if the status is not 2xx (except for specific negative tests) [Source: 1197].
• H0173 (Minimum Forensic Set):
    ◦ Answer: 3 Artifacts + Snapshots.
    ◦ Context: FILES_INDEX.json, CHUNK_INDEX.json, LEX_HITS.json, plus verifychain_after.json and health_before/after.json are mandated. Missing files = HALT [Source: 827, 1190].
• H0174 (Reproducible Zips):
    ◦ Answer: Standard Zip (Non-Deterministic).
    ◦ Context: zip -r captures timestamps, meaning hash equality relies on the file content remaining static. Re-zipping later results in a different hash. The Ledger records the upload hash as the source of truth [Source: 833, 1205].
• H0175 (Verification Drill):
    ◦ Answer: Automated in Pipeline.
    ◦ Context: The pipeline itself is the drill. Every deployment performs the "Upload -> Download -> Verify" cycle. It is not a separate event; it is the standard operating procedure [Source: 866, 1203].

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
🦅 P08 CI/CD, Tests, and Drift Gates (H0176–H0179)
• H0176 (CI Forbidden Strings):
    ◦ Answer: scan_repo.sh Gate.
    ◦ Context: The CI pipeline runs scripts/scan_repo.sh before deployment. This script uses rg (ripgrep) to hunt for forbidden patterns (secrets, "Gospel 445", blue hex codes). A single hit fails the build [Source: 906, 910].
• H0177 (Auth Negative Assertion):
    ◦ Answer: Strict 401/403 Only.
    ◦ Context: The orchestrator runs a request without headers. It explicitly checks if [[ "$code" =~ ^(401|403)$ ]]. If the result is 200 (bypass) or 500 (crash), the deployment HALTS [Source: 854, 874].
• H0178 (OPTIONS Preflight):
    ◦ Answer: Worker Contract Probe.
    ◦ Context: RC3 adds explicit probes for OPTIONS handling. It verifies the Worker returns 204 No Content and the correct CORS headers (Access-Control-Allow-Origin) without requiring authentication [Source: 892, 1311].
• H0179 (ALLOWED_ORIGIN Match):
    ◦ Answer: Integration Test Verification.
    ◦ Context: The integration test suite sends requests with various Origin headers to verify they are accepted/rejected according to the ALLOWED_ORIGIN env var configuration (Fail-Closed default) [Source: 217, 281].

Based on the Phoenix Flight Vault B archives (specifically PhoenixFlightVaultB-8.txt and The Phoenix 500 list), here is the continuation of the Audit Holes from H0210 through H0250.
This section completes Phase 09 (Admin UI Safety) and covers Phase 10 (Stage-4 Orchestrator Fragility), focusing on the specific risks of "Ghost Deployments," script drift between WSL/PowerShell, and evidence integrity.
  { id:"H0210", p:"P09", q:"Is the admin auth UX safe (never suggests placing keys in URLs)?" },
  { id:"H0211", p:"P09", q:"Does admin show request_id for every row and allow searching by it?" },
  { id:"H0212", p:"P09", q:"Does admin show chain verification status and last verified checkpoint?" },

Based on the Phoenix Flight, Vault, Gem, and Codex archives, here is the resolution matrix for holes H0210–H0239, reflecting the Phoenix Omega Monolith (v47.2-ULTIMA) configuration and the Stage 4 Orchestrator (RC3) protocols.
🦅 P09 Admin UI, Exposure, and Safety Defaults (H0210–H0225)
• H0210 (Admin Auth UX):
    ◦ Answer: Keyless / Header-Only.
    ◦ Context: The architecture mandates a "KEYLESS-BROWSER-UI". Secrets like PHOENIX_MASTER_KEY are never placed in URLs or client-side code. Admin actions are performed via CLI or a relay that injects the header server-side [Source: 1309, 1376, 1399].
• H0211 (Request ID in Admin):
    ◦ Answer: Mandatory Audit Field.
    ◦ Context: The Ledger schema requires a request_id column. The Admin UI ("Command Station") renders this field to allow tracing every row back to its ingestion logs [Source: 1469, 1515].
• H0212 (Chain Verification Status):
    ◦ Answer: HUD Status Integration.
    ◦ Context: The Admin HUD status panel (P05.S127) pulls from /health and /verifychain. It displays the current ledger_height and the integrity status (GREEN/RED) based on the latest Merkle fold [Source: 1154, 1594].
• H0213 (Oracle Timing Prevention):
    ◦ Answer: Constant-Time Equality (ctEq).
    ◦ Context: All key validations use crypto.subtle.timingSafeEqual (via the ctEq helper). The system refuses to return "early" on mismatched characters, preventing side-channel timing attacks [Source: 1176, 1181].
• H0214 (Caching Protection):
    ◦ Answer: no-store Headers.
    ◦ Context: Admin and Ledger endpoints explicitly set Cache-Control: no-store, max-age=0 to prevent edge or browser caching of sensitive state (P02.S30) [Source: 1533, 1563].
• H0215 (Pagination Stability):
    ◦ Answer: Cursor/ID-Based.
    ◦ Context: Pagination uses stable ordering (ORDER BY height DESC or id) to ensure consistent views even as new data is appended (P05.S124) [Source: 1481, 1593].
• H0216 (Filter Allowlist):
    ◦ Answer: Strict Schema.
    ◦ Context: Filters allowlisted to type, actor, and status. Arbitrary column filtering is rejected to prevent SQL injection or performance degradation (P05.S125) [Source: 1481, 1594].
• H0217 (PII Default):
    ◦ Answer: Masked/Redacted.
    ◦ Context: The "Face-Safe" mandate requires PII (names, faces) to be scrubbed before ledger entry. The Admin UI renders the redacted version by default [Source: 1293, 1474].
• H0218 (Gospel 444 Enforcement):
    ◦ Answer: Linter & Runtime Check.
    ◦ Context: A "Visual Audit" step (P05.S124) and runtime checks in /health verify the presence of Purple (#a855f7) and the absence of Blue (#0000ff). Violations trigger a halt [Source: 1390, 1598].
• H0219 (Panic Switch):
    ◦ Answer: Flash Freeze / Halt Latch.
    ◦ Context: A halt_state boolean in the Durable Object acts as a global kill-switch (P01.S24). If set to 1, all mutation endpoints return 423 Locked [Source: 1279, 1394].
• H0220 (Error Handling):
    ◦ Answer: Standard Envelope.
    ◦ Context: Errors return a JSON object { ok: false, error: "CODE", request_id: "..." }. Stack traces are suppressed in production to prevent leakage [Source: 1155, 1466].
• H0221 (Health Indicator):
    ◦ Answer: Active Probe.
    ◦ Context: The Admin dashboard polls /health (which checks D1/DO connectivity) and displays a live "Traffic Light" status (P05.S127) [Source: 1187, 1594].
• H0222 (Admin Access Audit):
    ◦ Answer: Ledgered Event.
    ◦ Context: Admin access events are logged to the audit table (or distinct ledger type) with actor_id, timestamp, and request_id [Source: 1498].
• H0223 (Route Namespace):
    ◦ Answer: Separation.
    ◦ Context: Admin routes are distinct (e.g., /admin or via separate Worker) and gated by x-sovereign-key, distinct from the public x-deathstar-key ingest path [Source: 1179, 1499].
• H0224 (Query Cap):
    ◦ Answer: Hard Limit.
    ◦ Context: Time range queries and result sets are capped (e.g., max 100 rows) to prevent DoS via massive database scans (H0129) [Source: 1480, 1500].
• H0225 (Safe Evidence Download):
    ◦ Answer: R2 Signed URLs / Proxy.
    ◦ Context: Evidence packs (ZIPs) are retrieved from R2 via secure, time-limited methods or proxied through the Worker, ensuring direct bucket access keys are never exposed [Source: 1500].

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
🦅 P10 Stage-4 Orchestrator & Script Fragility (H0226–H0239)
• H0226 (Environment Truth):
    ◦ Answer: WSL2 Mandate.
    ◦ Context: "ALL operations must now occur in WSL2 (Ubuntu)." Windows PowerShell usage for deployment scripts is deprecated/hardened (CVE-2025-54100 fix) but WSL2 is the "Truth Path" [Source: 1125, 1421].
• H0227 (OS Detection):
    ◦ Answer: Fail-Closed Checks.
    ◦ Context: Scripts like phoenix_stage4_run.sh use uname and check for bash capabilities. If the environment is unsupported, it halts immediately [Source: 1013, 1500].
• H0228 (Preflight Validation):
    ◦ Answer: Explicit preflight Function.
    ◦ Context: The script executes a preflight routine that validates PHOENIX_MASTER_KEY, WRANGLER_ENV, and tool availability (jq, curl, zip) before attempting any action [Source: 1146, 1500].
• H0229 (Deploy Sequence):
    ◦ Answer: Strict Order.
    ◦ Context: The PHOENIX_STAGE4_ULTIMATE_RUN.sh enforces: Secrets -> Deploy -> Migrations -> Auth Test. This prevents testing against stale or broken code [Source: 1296, 1500].
• H0230 (Wrangler Auth):
    ◦ Answer: wrangler whoami.
    ◦ Context: The script runs wrangler whoami (redacted in logs) or verifies the account ID to ensure the deploy targets the correct zone [Source: 1041, 1500].
• H0231 (HTTP Capture):
    ◦ Answer: http_json_strict Wrapper.
    ◦ Context: All curl calls are wrapped in functions that capture both the HTTP status code and the response body, failing if non-2xx (unless testing negative auth) [Source: 1144, 1500].
• H0232 (Auth Negative Test):
    ◦ Answer: Mandatory Gate.
    ◦ Context: The script sends a request without the auth header and asserts a 401 or 403 response. Any other code (200, 500) triggers a HALT [Source: 1141, 1501].
• H0233 (Version Confirmation):
    ◦ Answer: /health Git SHA.
    ◦ Context: The deploy_worker function validates that the /health endpoint returns the expected Git SHA/Version ID after deployment [Source: 1032, 1501].
• H0234 (Migration Confirmation):
    ◦ Answer: d1_sanity_migrations.
    ◦ Context: The script runs d1_sanity_migrations which checks if tables exist and migrations are applied, failing closed if the DB schema is invalid [Source: 1029, 1501].
• H0235 (D1 Binding Check):
    ◦ Answer: Startup Probe.
    ◦ Context: The Worker code (and script) verifies the D1 binding (env.DB) exists and is queryable (SELECT 1) during startup/health checks [Source: 1045, 1501].
• H0236 (DO Binding Check):
    ◦ Answer: Verified in Code.
    ◦ Context: Similar to D1, the PhoenixGovernor binding is verified. Missing bindings cause the Worker to throw on startup, detected by the post-deploy health check [Source: 1045, 1501].
• H0237 (CLI Determinism):
    ◦ Answer: Tool Version Capture.
    ◦ Context: The evidence pack includes wrangler --version and node -v output to detect drift in CLI syntax or behavior [Source: 1013, 1502].
• H0238 (Unique Run ID):
    ◦ Answer: RUN_LABEL / UUID.
    ◦ Context: A timestamped RUN_LABEL (e.g., audit_20260207T...) is generated at the start and used for all artifacts, logs, and the final ledger entry [Source: 1144, 1502].
• H0239 (Retry Logic):
    ◦ Answer: Transient Only.
    ◦ Context: The script uses retry logic for transient errors (network, 503) but never for validation failures or auth rejection (401/403) [Source: 1166, 1502].

Based on the Phoenix Flight, Vault, Plexy, and Codex archives, here is the resolution matrix for holes H0240–H0264, reflecting the Phoenix Omega Monolith (v47.2-ULTIMA) configuration and the Stage 4 Orchestrator (RC3) protocols.
🦅 P10 Stage-4 Orchestrator & Script Fragility (H0240–H0250)
• H0240 (Retry Limits & Backoff):
    ◦ Answer: Bounded (3x).
    ◦ Context: The script enforces MAX_RETRIES=3. For the specific case of Ledger Commit (408/429/5xx), the protocol mandates "3 immediate retries. No backoff. If failure persists → HALT" to prevent thundering herds or infinite spins (The Immortal Stitch) [Source: 1105, 1147, 1463].
• H0241 (Secret Scrubbing):
    ◦ Answer: Stdin Pipe & set +x.
    ◦ Context: Secrets are injected via stdin only (<<< "$SECRET") and never stored in variables printed by set -x. The script explicitly disables echo (set +x) during secret handling sections [Source: 1253, 1368].
• H0242 (Zip Secret Prevention):
    ◦ Answer: External Build & Redaction Scan.
    ◦ Context: The ZIP is built in a temporary directory outside the report directory to prevent recursion. Before zipping, a redaction scan (grep) runs on the source files; if SAFE_EVIDENCE=1, any hit halts the process [Source: 1268, 1329].
• H0243 (Repo Forbidden Strings):
    ◦ Answer: scan_repo.sh Gate.
    ◦ Context: The scan_repo.sh script runs before deployment. It greps for x-sovereign-key literals, 64-char hex strings, and Blue hex codes. A hit triggers immediate HALT [Source: 1326, 1338].
• H0244 (R2 Verification):
    ◦ Answer: Download & unzip -t.
    ◦ Context: The script does not trust the upload return code alone. It downloads the artifact immediately after upload and runs unzip -t to verify integrity and retrievability [Source: 1334, 1336].
• H0245 (Ledger Idempotency):
    ◦ Answer: BlockID = Zip SHA.
    ◦ Context: The Ledger Commit uses the evidence ZIP's SHA-256 hash as the blockid. The D1 Ledger rejects duplicate block IDs with 409 Conflict (interpreted as success), ensuring exactly one entry per artifact [Source: 1248, 1268].
• H0246 (Status Code Fail-Closed):
    ◦ Answer: Strict 2xx Check.
    ◦ Context: The http_json_strict wrapper captures the HTTP status code. If it is not 200 or 201 (or 409 for specific cases), the script exits with 1 (HALT). Auth checks specifically demand 401/403 [Source: 1248, 1325].
• H0247 (Summary Report):
    ◦ Answer: report.json.
    ◦ Context: The harvester emits a machine-readable report.json containing heights, summaries, and parameters. This file is hashed and included in the evidence ZIP [Source: 1069, 1334].
• H0248 (Auth Header Proof):
    ◦ Answer: CANON.json Binding.
    ◦ Context: The script imports HDR_NAME from CANON.json and uses it for all requests. The "Auth Negative Test" proves that requests without this specific header are rejected [Source: 1133, 1146].
• H0249 (Prod/Staging Guard):
    ◦ Answer: Explicit Env Vars.
    ◦ Context: The script requires WRANGLER_ENV to be explicitly set. It validates bucket names and DB names against the environment before execution to prevent cross-environment pollution [Source: 969, 1341].
• H0250 (Auditable Transcript):
    ◦ Answer: EVIDENCE_SEALED Ledger Entry.
    ◦ Context: The final step writes an EVIDENCE_SEALED event to the ledger, which references the zip_sha and run_label. This creates an immutable, timestamped record of the execution linked to the code artifacts [Source: 1311, 1340].

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
🦅 P11 Secrets, Leaks, Denylists, and Human Error (H0251–H0264)
• H0251 (Master Key Denylist):
    ◦ Answer: scan_repo.sh.
    ◦ Context: A dedicated script scans the repo for known secret patterns (e.g., PHOENIXMASTERKEY, x-deathstar-key) and specific compromised values (e.g., 754075Phoenix). It runs in CI and locally [Source: 1326, 1338].
• H0252 (High-Entropy Scan):
    ◦ Answer: Regex Heuristic.
    ◦ Context: The scan includes a regex for ([A-Za-z0-9_=-]{32,}) to catch high-entropy strings that look like keys or tokens [Source: 1327].
• H0253 (Bundle Scan):
    ◦ Answer: Repo Root Scan.
    ◦ Context: scan_repo.sh runs grep -R from the repository root. While node_modules is excluded, dist/ or built bundles are subject to the scan if present in the tree [Source: 1326, 1338].
• H0254 (Exception Scrubbing):
    ◦ Answer: Production Suppression.
    ◦ Context: In production, the Worker suppresses stack traces and returns standardized error envelopes (INTERNAL_ERROR) to prevent leaking environment variables or code paths [Source: 1155, 1460].
• H0255 (Wrangler Injection Only):
    ◦ Answer: Strict Policy.
    ◦ Context: "Secrets exist only as Wrangler secrets (never env files, never logs)." The wrangler.toml references variables, but the values are set via wrangler secret put [Source: 1001, 1019].
• H0256 (Zero-Downtime Rotation):
    ◦ Answer: Dual-Accept Window.
    ◦ Context: The rotation protocol involves adding a secondary valid key (PHOENIX_MASTER_KEY_2), waiting for propagation, switching clients, and then removing the old key (7-day window recommended) [Source: 1128, 1245].
• H0257 (Compromise Protocol):
    ◦ Answer: Kill Switch / Rotation.
    ◦ Context: The "Incident Ladder" defines LEVEL_3: KILL which requires immediate Sovereign Key rotation via wrangler secret put effectively invalidating the compromised credentials instantly [Source: 1232, 1257].
• H0258 (Least Privilege Tokens):
    ◦ Answer: Scoped Capabilities.
    ◦ Context: CI/CD uses CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN with specific scopes (Workers:Edit, Account:Read) rather than a global API key [Source: 923, 1004].
• H0259 (CLI Arg Prevention):
    ◦ Answer: Stdin Pipe.
    ◦ Context: Secrets are passed to scripts via environment variables or stdin (<<<), never as command-line arguments that would appear in ps or process lists [Source: 1028, 1253].
• H0260 (Script Echo Prevention):
    ◦ Answer: set +x.
    ◦ Context: Bash scripts explicitly disable execution tracing (set +x) around secret handling blocks to prevent secrets from being printed to logs [Source: 1253].
• H0261 (Stored Hashes):
    ◦ Answer: No Secret Storage in D1.
    ◦ Context: The system does not store passwords or secret hashes in D1. It relies on Cloudflare's secure environment variable storage. Payload hashes (SHA-256) are stored for integrity, but these are not auth secrets [Source: 752, 1406].
• H0262 (Env Separation):
    ◦ Answer: Wrangler Environments.
    ◦ Context: wrangler.toml defines separate [env.staging] and [env.production] sections. CI workflows use inputs to target the specific environment, ensuring secrets don't cross-pollinate [Source: 1018, 1024].
• H0263 (Local Dev Dummies):
    ◦ Answer: Explicit Export Required.
    ◦ Context: Local execution requires the user to explicitly export keys. There are no default keys committed to the repo, preventing accidental usage of prod keys in dev [Source: 1269, 1341].
• H0264 (Chat Hygiene):
    ◦ Answer: "NO SECRETS IN CHAT".
    ◦ Context: This is a "Hard Mandate." Operators are forbidden from pasting raw secrets into the LLM context. Placeholder values (e.g., PHOENIX_MASTER_KEY) must be used instead [Source: 1213, 1369].
