Based on the Phoenix Flight, Vault, Plexy, and Codex archives, here is the resolution matrix for holes H0265–H0300.
This section completes Phase 11 (Secrets & Governance) and covers Phase 12 (Rate Limiting & Abuse), reflecting the Phoenix Omega Monolith (v47.2-ULTIMA) configuration.
🦅 P11 Secrets, Leaks, Denylists, and Human Error (H0265–H0275)
• H0265 (Request Body Logging):
    ◦ Answer: Payload Fingerprinting (SHA-256).
    ◦ Context: The "NO SECRETS IN CHAT" mandate forbids logging raw payloads. Instead, the system logs the SHA-256 hash of the payload (payload_hash) to prove receipt and integrity without exposing content [Source: 1261, 1492].
• H0266 (Admin Export Safety):
    ◦ Answer: Explicit Column Allowlist.
    ◦ Context: Admin exports utilize a strict SQL SELECT list (e.g., id, type, timestamp, hash) rather than SELECT *. Sensitive columns (if any exist in future expansions) are excluded by default [Source: 1492].
• H0267 (Error Response Hygiene):
    ◦ Answer: Standard Envelope (ERROR_CODES).
    ◦ Context: Errors return a fixed enum code (e.g., CONFIG_MISSING) rather than variable names (e.g., MISSING_ENV_VAR_PHOENIX_KEY). This prevents internal structure leakage [Source: 1466, 1493].
• H0268 (Secret Format Validator):
    ◦ Answer: Hex64 Regex Check.
    ◦ Context: The preflight check in deployment scripts validates that PHOENIX_MASTER_KEY matches ^[a-f0-9]{64}$ (or the specific 32-char high-entropy format). Invalid formats halt the deploy [Source: 1014, 1493].
• H0269 (Secret Presence Check):
    ◦ Answer: Boolean Status Endpoint.
    ◦ Context: The /health endpoint checks if (env.PHOENIX_MASTER_KEY) and returns auth_configured: true/false, never the key itself [Source: 1493].
• H0270 (Rotation Event Logging):
    ◦ Answer: SYSTEM_ROTATION Event.
    ◦ Context: Key rotations are manual but must be followed by a ledger entry: { type: "SYSTEM_ROTATION", actor: "AGENT-100", reason: "SCHEDULED" }. This creates an audit trail for access changes [Source: 1493].
• H0271 (Old Key Invalidation):
    ◦ Answer: 7-Day Hard Stop.
    ◦ Context: The migration protocol defines a "Dual-Accept Window" of 7 days. After this, the code is updated to remove the legacy alias (PHOENIXMASTERKEY), strictly enforcing the new key [Source: 1141, 1493].
• H0272 (Global Kill-Switch):
    ◦ Answer: halt_state Latch.
    ◦ Context: If halt_state is set to 1 in the Durable Object, all keys (including valid ones) are rejected with 423 LOCKED. This is the ultimate "Stop-The-Line" mechanism [Source: 1279, 1494].
• H0273 (Read vs. Write Keys):
    ◦ Answer: Separation of Duties.
    ◦ Context:
        ▪ Read/Admin: x-sovereign-key (Internal/Admin tools).
        ▪ Write/Mutation: x-deathstar-key (Public Ingest).
        ▪ This limits the blast radius if the ingestion key is leaked [Source: 1140, 1494].
• H0274 (Operator Identity):
    ◦ Answer: actor_id Binding.
    ◦ Context: Every request must include an actor_id in the payload. While currently self-asserted for "Mouse" users, Admin actions bind the x-sovereign-key to the AGENT-100 identity [Source: 1494].
• H0275 (Tamper-Evident Config):
    ◦ Answer: Signed Manifests.
    ◦ Context: Configuration changes (e.g., wrangler.toml) are committed to git. The deployment script captures the git commit hash and seals it in the EVIDENCE_SEALED ledger entry [Source: 1494].

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
🦅 P12 Rate Limiting, Abuse, and Resource Exhaustion (H0276–H0300)
• H0276 (Rate Limit Model):
    ◦ Answer: Token Bucket (Durable Object).
    ◦ Context: A dedicated RateLimiter Durable Object maintains token buckets. It is consistent across all edge locations, preventing "distributed counter drift" common in stateless workers [Source: 201, 1466].
• H0277 (Rate Limit Keying):
    ◦ Answer: Combined Strategy.
    ◦ Context:
        ▪ Public: Keyed by IP Address.
        ▪ Authenticated: Keyed by x-deathstar-key (or tenant ID).
        ▪ Sovereign: Bypasses limits (RPSLIMIT override) [Source: 212, 1466].
• H0278 (Burst & Retry-After):
    ◦ Answer: Standard Headers.
    ◦ Context: Responses include x-rate-limit-remaining, x-rate-limit-reset, and on 429, a Retry-After header indicating seconds to wait [Source: 200, 1466].
• H0279 (Admin Limits):
    ◦ Answer: Strict Separation.
    ◦ Context: Admin endpoints (/admin) use a separate, tighter rate limit to prevent brute-force login attempts, distinct from the high-throughput ingestion pipe [Source: 1466].
• H0280 (Expensive Query Protection):
    ◦ Answer: Concurrency Lock.
    ◦ Context: /verifychain (a heavy O(n) operation) is guarded by a concurrency limit (e.g., max 1 concurrent verification) to prevent CPU starvation of the ingestion loop [Source: 1466].
• H0281 (Time Range Cap):
    ◦ Answer: Hard Logic Cap.
    ◦ Context: Ledger queries enforce a maximum time window (e.g., 7 days) or row count (LIMIT 100) to prevent massive D1 scans that could time out the Worker [Source: 1467].
• H0282 (Batch Ingest Cap):
    ◦ Answer: Item Count Limit.
    ◦ Context: Batch endpoints reject arrays larger than 50 items. This ensures the transaction stays within the D1 write timeout limits [Source: 1467].
• H0283 (Write Volume Cap):
    ◦ Answer: D1 Quota Guard.
    ◦ Context: The RateLimiter DO tracks writes per minute. If a single key exceeds the "Safety Ceiling" (e.g., 600 writes/min), it triggers a 429 to protect the underlying database [Source: 1467].
• H0284 (Response Size Cap):
    ◦ Answer: 1MB Output Limit.
    ◦ Context: To protect downstream clients and bandwidth, the Worker truncates or errors if a response body generation exceeds MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES [Source: 1467].
• H0285 (Admin HTML Cap):
    ◦ Answer: Pagination Forced.
    ◦ Context: The Admin UI never renders "all rows." It enforces pagination (50 rows/page) to ensure the HTML payload remains small and renderable [Source: 1467].
• H0286 (Recursion Depth):
    ◦ Answer: JSON Parse Limit.
    ◦ Context: While standard JSON.parse is used, the input validation step rejects objects with excessive nesting depth (if a validator like Zod is configured with depth limits) to prevent stack overflows [Source: 1467].
• H0287 (Backpressure):
    ◦ Answer: 503 Service Unavailable.
    ◦ Context: If the Durable Object is overloaded (overloaded exception), the Worker returns 503 immediately, signaling load balancers or clients to back off [Source: 1468].
• H0288 (Upstream Circuit Breaker):
    ◦ Answer: Timeout & Fail-Fast.
    ◦ Context: Calls to external AI APIs (Gemini/Perplexity) are wrapped in Promise.race with a tight timeout. If they fail/timeout repeatedly, the system can temporarily disable that provider [Source: 1468].
• H0289 (Abuse Ban):
    ◦ Answer: Ephemeral Jail.
    ◦ Context: The RateLimiter DO can promote an IP to "Jail" (blocked for 24h) if it hits the rate limit >10 times in a short window [Source: 1468].
• H0290 (Auth Timing):
    ◦ Answer: Uniform Duration.
    ◦ Context: Auth checks use ctEq (constant time). Furthermore, 401 responses are delayed to match the average processing time of a successful auth check if necessary (though ctEq usually suffices) [Source: 1468].
• H0291 (Streaming Reject):
    ◦ Answer: Header-First Check.
    ◦ Context: Content-Length is checked before reading the body stream. If >1MB, the request is rejected immediately with 413 Payload Too Large without consuming bandwidth [Source: 1468].
• H0292 (Storage Quotas):
    ◦ Answer: Monitoring Drone.
    ◦ Context: A "Cost Monitor" drone checks D1 row counts and R2 usage daily. It alerts via the "Mission Dashboard" if growth trends predict a quota breach [Source: 1469].
• H0293 (Budget Burn Alert):
    ◦ Answer: Threshold Trigger.
    ◦ Context: Alerts fire if daily "Requests" or "Duration" exceed 80% of the free/pro tier allocation [Source: 1469].
• H0294 (Retry Loop Prevention):
    ◦ Answer: Max-Retry Counter.
    ◦ Context: All internal retry logic (CAS-Retry) has a hard MAX_ATTEMPTS (3). It explicitly forbids infinite loops [Source: 1469].
• H0295 (Abuse Simulation):
    ◦ Answer: Chaos-07 Drill.
    ◦ Context: The simulate_attack.js script floods the staging environment with malformed, oversized, and auth-less requests to verify the rate limiter and rejection logic hold up [Source: 1469].
• H0296 (Suspicious Payload Policy):
    ◦ Answer: Strict JSON Only.
    ◦ Context: Content-Type must be application/json. text/plain or application/x-www-form-urlencoded are rejected with 415 Unsupported Media Type [Source: 1469].
• H0297 (Durable Rate Counters):
    ◦ Answer: Yes (DO Persistence).
    ◦ Context: The RateLimiter is a Durable Object, meaning its state (counters) persists to disk. A Worker restart does not reset the rate limits [Source: 1470].
• H0298 (Rate Limit Auditing):
    ◦ Answer: Redacted Logs.
    ◦ Context: When a user is rate-limited, a log entry is created: { type: "RATE_LIMIT_HIT", ip_hash: "...", endpoint: "..." }. The raw IP is hashed [Source: 1470].
• H0299 (Manual Override):
    ◦ Answer: Ledgered Config Update.
    ◦ Context: Increasing a limit requires a deployment config change or a specific x-sovereign-key command to the Governor DO to update the policy dynamically [Source: 1470].
• H0300 (Stop-Line on Abuse):
    ◦ Answer: Global Defcon.
    ◦ Context: If error rates (4xx/5xx) exceed 10% of traffic, the system can auto-trigger INCIDENT-2 (FREEZE), rejecting all non-admin traffic until reviewed [Source: 1470].

# 🦅 PHOENIX OMEGA MONOLITH (V47.2-ULTIMA)

# STATUS: FAIL-CLOSED | ZERO-DRIFT
